# Proposed OEIS comments: A267882 to A270052

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A267882

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267882>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [431](../papers/00001-00500/00431-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 22 2016 and Apr 20 2019: (Start)
> Conjectured e.g.f.: exp(x)*(x^2 + 3*x - 5) + x^2/2 + 3*x + 6. - _Stefano Spezia_, Sep 08 2025

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence follows from the generating function this entry already states as fact: a recurrence with constant coefficients is exactly a denominator of the generating function, so the two are the same statement, and it holds for n > 2. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A267906

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267906>  
Result obtained: **3 September 2026**  
Paper: [3379](../papers/03001-03500/03379-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 14*a(n-1) - 60*a(n-2) + 50*a(n-3) + 145*a(n-4) - 80*a(n-5) - 84*a(n-6) + 16*a(n-7).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(1 - 7*x + 23*x^2 - 15*x^3 - 34*x^4 - 14*x^5 + 4*x^6) / ((1 - x)*(1 + x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 - 6*x + x^2)*(1 - 4*x - 4*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 25 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 185 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 7. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical g.f.: 3*x*(1 - x - 18*x^2 + 36*x^3 + 72*x^4 - 144*x^5) / ((1 + 3*x)*(1 - 4*x)*(1 - 7*x + 9*x^2)*(1 - 2*x - 12*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Mar 21 2018

## A267953

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A267953>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11762](../papers/11501-12000/11762-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 56 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1688 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 56, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268554

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268554>  
Result obtained: **30 August 2026**  
Paper: [11294](../papers/11001-11500/11294-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjecture: n^3*(2*n-1)*a(n) -6*(4*n-1)*(3*n-1)*(3*n-2)*(4*n-3)*a(n-1)=0. - _R. J. Mathar_, Mar 10 2016
> For integer r and positive integer s, define sequences {u(n) : n >= 0} and {v(n) : n >= 0} by setting u(n) = [x^(s*n)] F(x)^(r*n) and v(n) = [x^(s*n)] E(x)^(r*n). We conjecture that both u(n) and v(n) satisfy the above supercongruences. (End)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This follows from the closed form already on the entry. It is a sum of finitely many hypergeometric terms, so every shift quotient is a rational function of n and the recurrence collapses to one rational identity in n, which is true. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268788

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268788>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11621](../papers/11501-12000/11621-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A268892

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A268892>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11622](../papers/11501-12000/11622-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269001

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269001>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11623](../papers/11501-12000/11623-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A269088

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269088>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [11624](../papers/11501-12000/11624-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 68 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 177 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 68, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269614>

> Empirical g.f.: x*(4 - 32*x + 76*x^2 - 27*x^3 - 54*x^4 - 22*x^5 - 7*x^6) / ((1 - 3*x)*(1 - 9*x + 24*x^2 - 9*x^3 - 24*x^4 - 9*x^5 - 3*x^6)). - _Colin Barker_, Jan 25 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. ```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A269762>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [4027](../papers/04001-04500/04027-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 184*a(n-1) - 9134*a(n-2) + 61640*a(n-3) - 112225*a(n-4) for n>5.
> Empirical g.f.: 60*x*(1 + 108*x - 850*x^2 + 1554*x^3 + 225*x^4) / (1 - 92*x + 335*x^2)^2. - _Colin Barker_, Jan 28 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 432 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 5. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
